DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00403 | Low Temperature and Low Pressure Fine-pitch Cu-Cu Bonding by Electroplated In-Sn-Passivation
To meet the demands of advanced high-density interconnects emerging from data centers and electronics, Cu–Cu bonding has been widely adopted as a primary bonding solution. Within this domain, solid–liquid interdiffusion (SLID) bonding is regarded as a key technology to replace traditional solder with full intermetallic compound (IMC) joints, which are reported to have better electromigration (EM) performance. By evolving the conventional Cu–Sn binary platform, this work explores a novel In–Sn passivation structure with a diffusion barrier to suppress interdiffusion between Cu and In prior to bonding. All metal layers were fabricated by a wet-chemical process. The primary objective is to optimize the bonding window and enable reliable interconnects at reduced temperatures and lower pressures. Several analysis, including cross-sectional focused ion beam (FIB), scanning electron microscopy (SEM), energy-dispersive spectroscopy (EDS), and electrical tests, were conducted to understand the reaction of the proposed structure and joint properties. Finally, a brand-new, fully wet-chemical, CMP-free Cu–Cu bonding process with In–Sn passivation was developed without the need for high vacuum.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00203 | Advanced Manufacturing Solutions for Large Form Factor Patch on Interposer (PoINT) Scaling

The work presents the development of a weighted reflow process that applies controlled mechanical loading to the patch and interposer during solder melting to constrain dynamic warpage, promote uniform solder joint collapse, and stabilize chip gap height while preserving solder driven self-alignment. DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00406 | High Fidelity Reliability Prediction of SAC Solder Under Drop Impact via High Strain Rate and Temperature Dependent Properties

Dog bone-shaped specimens were fabricated for both materials using SHPB tensile tests at strain-rate range of 600-1500 /s to obtain dynamic stress-strain responses. The FEM simulations incorporating SHPB-based high strain-rate properties predicted the drop failure life within 8 % of the experiment results. These results demonstrate that SHPB-based high strain-rate characterization of both solder and PCB is essential for accurate board-level drop failure life prediction. The proposed approach establishes an experimental-FEM correlation between high strain-rate material behavior and solder joint drop reliability.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00260 | Experimental Studies of Assembly Material Creep Behaviors and The Impact to Package Warpage

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00261 | Layout-Driven FEM for Thermal and Thermo-Mechanical Analysis of MPGA and BGA packages
This paper presents a layout-driven finite element methodology for thermal and thermo-mechanical analysis of advanced semiconductor packages. As packaging complexity increases, conventional simulation approaches face limitations: uniform-property models fail to capture layout-dependent behavior, while full geometry reconstruction from detailed design data is computationally expensive. To address this gap, the proposed workflow leverages design layout information to enable realistic full-chip analysis with practical computational cost.The methodology begins by extracting pattern density from GDS or OASIS layout data at a resolution of approximately 100 μm. Based on this density, high-resolution material property maps are generated. Thermal simulations employ spatially varying thermal conductivity maps, while stress and warpage simulations use effective Young’s modulus and Poisson’s ratio maps. These layer-specific property maps are stacked within the finite element model to represent BEOL structures, interconnect networks, and bump arrays across the full chip. The simulation framework enables prediction of localized temperature and stress distributions, as well as global metrics such as thermal resistance and package warpage.Compared with conventional averaging approaches, the proposed method captures spatial variations more accurately while maintaining efficient turnaround time. In contrast to explicit geometry reconstruction, it enables faster system-level evaluation, making it suitable for early design iterations. The workflow also supports a shift-left strategy by allowing design-stage analysis based on layout data, while remaining compatible with later-stage refinement using manufacturing data such as SEM-based topography.The methodology is applicable to MPGA and BGA packages and is extensible to emerging architectures, including backside power delivery networks (BSPDN). Overall, this layout-driven approach provides a scalable and practical platform for thermal management, warpage prediction, and reliability assessment in advanced semiconductor packaging.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00267 | FEA-Integrated 3PB And BOR Testing For Fracture Strength Characterization Of Ultra-thin Dies: Effects Of Thickness, Surface Conditions, And Anisotropy

In particular, an accurate method for measuring ultra-thin die strength that accounts for large deformation and anisotropic material properties is required. In this study, a finite element analysis (FEA)-integrated strength measurement method using three-point bending (3PB) and ball-on-ring (BOR) tests was developed. Fracture strength was determined by extracting the maximum principal stress from FEA simulations at crack origins identified through scanning electron microscopy (SEM) fractography. This validated framework enables accurate strength assessment and provides guidance for process optimization to improve packaging yield and reliability.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00101 | 3D Integration of an SRAM chiplet in Fan-Out Embedded Bridge Platform Achieving Low Energy Read/Write
The ability to disaggregate large systems into chiplets using advanced packaging has provided an alternate path for continuing the scaling of state-of-the-art computer systems. In the context of memory systems, extending the memory capacity of a system with disaggregated memory chiplets requires a chiplet integration platform with high bandwidth connectivity and low energy overhead. Organic fan-out advanced packages such as fan-out embedded bridge can be leveraged to obtain a wide interconnect plane for 3D integration of memory chiplets.In this paper, we demonstrate the 3D vertical integration of an SRAM chiplet embedded in a fan-out embedded bridge advanced package, achieving a bandwidth of 265 GB/s/mm2 at <0.24 pJ/bit in 50:50 read/write workloads. The embedded memory die is interconnected to a top die SoC through a dense microbump interconnect matrix with 25 µm pitch. An electrical analysis of the datapath reveals that the die-to-die connectivity accounts for less than 15% of the total power, while overall on-die data movement accounted for 30% of the total power. A further reduction in energy per bit can be achieved at lower frequency, reaching 0.15 pJ/bit for an overall read/write bandwidth of 166 GB/s/mm2.This result demonstrates the feasibility of implementing memory functions in the embedded dies of organic packages and provides an additional design vector for system integration while meeting the modern system requirements for high bandwidth and low energy.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00290 | System-Level Framework for Co-Optimizing Electromigration and Voltage Drop in High-Power Packages
The escalating power demands of high-performance computing (HPC) and artificial intelligence (AI) chips have intensified voltage drop and electromigration (EM) risks in ball grid arrays (BGAs), threatening chip reliability. While existing solutions focus on package-level simulations or material enhancements, this work identifies critical gaps: idealized source assumptions and overlooked board-package interactions. We propose a resistor-based co-simulation methodology to model integrated systems using distributed ports, addressing current crowding effects neglected in lumped models. Our experiments demonstrate that distributed modeling reveals 3× higher peak currents (7.38A vs. 2A) and 9× EM lifetime reduction versus optimistic lumped approaches. Design optimizations—including power plane expansion (>2.5× width for 40% peak current reduction), multi-directional VRM placement (43.5% voltage drop improvement), and ground-to-power ball ratio adjustments—collectively mitigate EM-voltage drop trade-offs. These findings establish that holistic package-board co-design is indispensable for next-generation power integrity management.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00127 | Multiscale Mechanical Characterization of Hybrid-Bonding Copper: From Instrumented Indentation to AFM
As transistor dimensions approach their physical scaling limits, advanced packaging has emerged as an effective solution to today’s challenging demands. Hybrid bonding (HB), in particular, has become a disruptive enabler in this space. With interconnect pitches below 10 µm and trending submicron, the influence of length-scale effects cannot be overlooked as the behavior of metals at these scales deviates from that predicted by bulk properties. In this context, understanding, modeling, and optimizing HB-systems for reliability requires knowledge of elastoplastic mechanical properties at the micro- and nanoscale.Here, we present a framework for multiscale mechanical characterization of polycrystalline, electroplated copper (Cu) surfaces in HB-ready patterns under ambient conditions. At the microscale, we leveraged the instrumented nanoindentation technique (IIT) with spherical probes of 1 µm and 5 µm nominal radii, near the critical dimension of the metal pads. At the nanoscale, we employed contact resonance and indentation via atomic force microscopy (AFM) with spherical probe radii of less than 30 nm, enabling localized probing of nanoscale features. Using single-step indentation we first examined the stochastic, earliest onset of plastic deformation (i.e., incipient plasticity). In complement, we then harness multi-step indentation to derive indentation stress-strain (ISS) curves that reflect later stages of plasticity (i.e., developed plasticity) and from which indentation yield stress is obtained. Our experiments reveal a marked difference in the incipient plasticity of Cu, with AFM approaching the theoretical limit (τy ≈ 8.7 GPa), and IIT revealing lower strength limits (τy ≈ 0.75 GPa). ISS curves show an indentation yield stress of (σy ≈ 0.64-0.83 GPa), comparable to results from micropillar compression. Our findings emphasize the importance of length-scale relevant metrology, which is critical for modeling the response of Cu under different loading scenarios (e.g., nanoscopic contact at bonding, thermal warping). Moreover, our novel multimodal AFM methodology provides an effective approach for elastoplastic characterization aligned with the industry's drive for smaller pitches, in-situ testing, and the widespread availability of AFM technology. Concurrently, to facilitate the adoption of our framework, we introduce an open-source software for the analysis of micro- and nanoscale spherical indentation experiments.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00250 | Dependence of Leakage Current Characteristics on Bonding Dielectrics in Fine-Pitch Hybrid Cu Bonding
Hybrid Cu bonding has emerged as a key enabling technology for high density three dimensional integration in next generation semiconductor devices. As bonding pitches scale down to the submicron scale, subtle variations in the bonding process and bonding dielectric properties can directly influence leakage current behavior through the bonding layer. In this study, a fast and non destructive leakage current based electrical analysis methodology is proposed to evaluate bonding layer integrity in fine pitch hybrid Cu bonding. A large scale array consisting of 20,000 bonding pads was used. The origin of leakage current was verified through bonding misalignment analysis, establishing leakage current as a process sensitive electrical metrology signal. Using this approach, the electrical behaviors of silicon carbonitride and silicon dioxide bonding layers were quantitatively compared in patterned hybrid Cu bonding structures. Silicon carbonitride bonding layers were dominated by a Poole Frenkel conduction mechanism, resulting in a pronounced increase in leakage current with applied electric field, whereas silicon dioxide bonding layers maintained comparatively low leakage current levels and a wide electrical margin under the tested conditions. In addition, unbiased highly accelerated stress tests were performed as a supplementary accelerated condition to examine bonding dielectrics under high temperature and high humidity environments. The stress tests were conducted at 130 degrees Celsius and 85 percent relative humidity for up to 14 days, and no abrupt changes in leakage behavior were observed within the tested duration. Overall, this study establishes a scalable leakage current based electrical metrology framework for quantitatively assessing bonding layer integrity in fine pitch hybrid Cu bonding. The proposed framework provides a practical diagnostic tool for evaluating bonding dielectric dependent electrical behavior in next generation three dimensional integrated circuits.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00104 | Low-cost process of organic RDL-based packaging platform enabling UCIe 32 GT/s link speed
We propose a low-cost process for fabricating an organic redistribution layer (RDL)-based 2.5D package to satisfy the maximum data rate of the universal chiplet interconnect express (UCIe) 2.0 specifications. The RDL is the core part of the proposed package, and six RDLs can be produced using a low-cost semi-additive process (SAP) without a chemical–mechanical polishing (CMP) process. To prepare a package for measuring the data rate, two system-on-a-chip (SoC) dies made using 3 nm CMOS technology were interconnected through an interposer-type RDL. In the RDL process, the minimum flatness has to be obtained for the intermetal dielectric (IMD), which was formed by curing a photo-imageable dielectric (PID) material. Therefore, we focused on the selection of a PID with little shrinkage during the curing process. When our selected PID was used with the optimized process, the accumulated flatness was too small to be measured after a total six RDLs were formed. The 2.5D package with interposer-type RDLs was assembled using the our previously reported method that uses a "molded bridge with top and bottom interconnection (MBTBI)" process, where a conventional package process can be applied. A signal-integrity (SI) simulation predicted a 32 GT/s data rate for the package with six RDLs, a line/spacing (L/S) of 2/2 µm, and a channel length of 2.2 mm between two SoC dies. The speed was maintained even with a 20% variation in the main physical parameters of the RDL. This study demonstrates that our low-cost SAP-based interposer package achieves a speed that is well above the specification from UCIe 2.0 and ensures reliable signal integrity despite process variations.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00322 | Trace-Element Effects in SAC Alloys: Mitigating HIP Defects in 5G/Automotive SoC SMT Assemblies
Head-in-Pillow (HIP) defects remain a critical challenge in ball grid array (BGA) packaging for surface mount technology (SMT), particularly in advanced assemblies such as 5G SoC and automotive/high-performance computing modules. HIP not only reduces assembly yield but also threatens long-term reliability in mission-critical applications. While HIP is conventionally attributed to package warpage, surface contamination, or oxidation, this explanation is incomplete. In this study, deliberately designed small-package test vehicles (2mm × 2mm) were employed to suppress package warpage noise. However, significant HIP defects were still observed, which suggests additional mechanisms beyond warpage. In this work, we investigated the overlooked trace-element effects in Sn-Ag-Cu (SAC) solder alloys. Specifically, the influence of phosphorus (P) and germanium (Ge) additives on HIP formation was systematically studied. SAC solder balls with controlled levels of P and Ge were subjected to accelerated aging tests. Following JEDEC J-STD-002 guidelines, the packages underwent steam aging at 93°C/100%RH for 8 hours (equivalent to ~1 year of field exposure under normal storage conditions), followed by board-level reflow. During reflow, soldering behavior was directly monitored in real time using an SMT scope, enabling in-situ observation of joint formation, and interfacial microstructures were subsequently characterized by after reflow. Results revealed that P exhibits a dualistic effect on wetting behavior. Auger Electron Spectroscopy (AES) results showed that 30 ppm (0.003%) P reduced the SnOx layer thickness to 7.1 nm compared to 11.8 nm without P, outperforming Ge in oxidation resistance. However, P also significantly accelerated the formation of Cu6Sn5 intermetallic compounds (IMCs) on solder surfaces. After steam aging and reflow, these IMCs acted as barriers at the solder-pad interface, elevating HIP incidence. By contrast, Ge provided more balanced behavior: it moderately suppressed oxidation without promoting excessive IMC formation, thereby mitigating HIP under identical conditions. Quantitatively, the HIP defect rate showed a dramatic change with P content: at 30 ppm (0.003%) P the HIP dppm increased to 140,000, compared to near-zero for baseline SAC. By optimizing the alloy composition with controlled Ge additions, the HIP dppm was reduced by orders of magnitude, demonstrating a practical materials-level pathway for reliability improvement. These results demonstrate that HIP formation in SMT assembly is governed not only by macroscopic factors such as package warpage, but also by trace-element-controlled interfacial reaction pathways. The present study provides fundamental insight into the coupled effects of oxidation suppression and intermetallic compound evolution on solder joint integrity in advanced electronic packaging.

DOI 10.1109/ECTC51846.2026.00082 | Figures of Merit to Characterize the Signal Integrity Performance of Interposer Interconnect for High Bandwidth Memory (HBM)
This paper presents a novel framework for analyzing signal integrity (SI) characteristics of interposer interconnects for high bandwidth memories (HBMs). Two figures of merit (FoMs) are proposed as criteria to distinguish between RC- and LC-dominance of interposer interconnects and to identify the interposer interconnect length where the inter-symbol interference (ISI) effect due to signal overshoot becomes critical. The first figure of merit compares the interconnect’s total resistance (Rtot) and characteristic impedance (Z0) to distinguish whether the interposer interconnect acts as an on-chip RC interconnect (RC-dominance) or as a transmission line (LC-dominance). The second figure of merit, which is particularly valid within the LC-dominant region, compares the unit interval (UI) with the signal settling time following the signal overshoot caused by the impedance mismatch between the driver resistance (Ron) and the characteristic impedance (Z0). Finally, the SI optimization strategies derived from this analysis are presented to achieve eye aperture exceeding 60%UI for 30 Gbps next-generation HBM interfaces.

DOI 10.1109/EPTC67330.2025.11392582 | Enhancing Wafer Bonding Strength Via Surface and Dielectric Modification Using Plasma Activation Process
This study systematically investigates the mechanisms by which plasma treatment modes influence wafer bonding strength in Silicon Carbon Nitride (SiCN) dielectrics. Both Remote and Reactive Ion Etching (RIE) O2 plasma treatments were applied, and their effects on surface chemistry, subsurface modifications, and bulk moisture interactions were evaluated using TEM, ToF-SIMS, XPS, and MEIS analyses. RIE plasma treatments were found to progressively increase bonding strength with longer exposure times, due to subsurface ion-induced damage that facilitates water uptake and subsequent silanol-to-siloxane conversion. In contrast, Remote plasma treatments promote surface hydroxylation, enhancing initial bonding strength, but extended exposure reduces bonding efficacy by diminishing surface -OH density. Incorporation of low-k layers beneath the bonding dielectric further improves bonding strength by enabling controlled moisture absorption and enhancing siloxane network formation, particularly in thinner and more porous upper dielectric layers. Dense dielectrics limit this effect due to restricted water diffusion. These results reveal a complex interplay between surface chemistry, plasma-induced subsurface modifications, and bulk moisture properties, offering actionable insights for the design of dielectric stacks and plasma activation strategies to achieve mechanically robust W 2 W and D 2 W bonds.

DOI 10.1109/EPTC67330.2025.11392617 | Evaluating Dummy Die Sizes and Compound Adjustments to Reduce Wafer Warpage in FOEB-T Packaging
Wafer warpage remains a criterial issue in advanced FO-EB-T (Fan-Out Embedded Bridge with TSV) packaging, impacting die-to-wafer bonding accuracy and overall manufacturing yield. This study systematically investigates the influence of dummy die size and compound properties on phenomenon of wafer warpage, employing a combination of finite-element modeling and comprehensive experimental data to derive meaningful insights. Three distinct dummy die sizes were rigorously tested across various compound types, with warpage measured at each major step to assess its effect on the overall assembly. The Simulation results demonstrated that reducing the dummy die size leads to a significant reduction in wafer warpage, thereby enabling the packaging process to meet criteria at the critical C4 step. Furthermore, the compound studies indicated that enhancing the coefficient of thermal expansion (CTE) of the molding compound proved to be a more effective for warpage reduction compared to merely increasing the modulus of the materials used. The utilization of high CTE compound, in conjunction with the implementation of small dummy dies, produced the most favorable outcomes, with wafer warpage measurements comfortably within the specified limits. In addition to these findings, strategies were meticulously evaluated to address the prevalence of defective RDL (Redistribution Layer) areas, which can randomly affect up to 10% of the wafer. The simulation data indicated that attaching a large dummy die to the affected areas achieves comparable warpage control to the standard configuration, thus avoiding unnecessary wastage of functional dies and maintaining the reliability of overall manufacturing process. Overall, the results highlight the importance of optimizing dummy die size and the CTE compound used, while also employing intelligent die allocation strategies for defective RDL areas. These combined approaches can effectively minimize wafer warpage and significantly enhance yield in FOEB-T packaging processes, contributing to more efficient and reliable semiconductor manufacturing.

As a result, implementing Design for Reliability (DfR) practices to ensure the reliability of 3D NAND in SSDs, particularly focusing on thermal reliability, has become crucial. Finite Element Analysis (FEA) was utilized to determine the failure location and calculate the plastic work density per cycle to evaluate the fatigue life under temperature loading conditions. [1] –[4] This study explored design prospects to assess their impact on NAND solder joint reliability (SJR) in SSDs. Advancements in SSDs can be made by analyzing FEA modeling and testing data for more durable development and manufacturing.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353563 | Comprehensive Evaluation of Challenges in Realizing Disturb-Free Ferroelectric Vertical NAND via String-Compatible Independent Pass Gates
In this work, we present comprehensive experimental and modeling studies addressing the challenges of our previously proposed dual-port ferroelectric FET (FeFET) architecture for pass disturb-free vertical NAND storage. This architecture incorporates a string-compatible independent pass gate within the string core, which is dedicated for pass operation rather than relying on the same write gate. Our key findings are as follows: i) The non-ferroelectric pass gate ensures pass disturb immunity via its geometry—pass voltage aids retention of the high-threshold voltage (HVT) state, while the its adverse influence on the low-threshold voltage (LVT) state is effectively screened by the channel; ii) A critical challenge is the increased leakage current in the HVT state, attributed to a weak back-channel region not fully depleted by the front gate, which is verified via both TCAD modeling and experimental demonstration; iii) To mitigate this, we explore the design space of the pass gate oxide and channel thickness through TCAD simulations, and experimentally validate the modeling using dual-port gate-side injection (GSI) FeFETs; iv) The example design, when implemented, demonstrates pass disturb-free operation while effectively managing the leakage trade-off; v) Another challenge is reduced ON-current for LVT state when unselected cells remain in HVT states. This can be mitigated by applying proper pass gate biases.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353558 | DAC-Free p-bits: Asynchronous Self-Coloring and On-Chip Annealing
Since their inception, nanodevice implementations of probabilistic bits (p-bits) have typically relied on analog-input, digital-output designs, where an analog bias tunes the output of a stochastic nanodevice. This analog requirement hinders scalability because it requires either expensive digital-to-analog-converters (DAC) or analog connectivity through additional nanotechnologies such as resistive crossbars, both of which are undesirable in the short term. Here, we present a DAC-free, digital input digital output p-bit architecture that solves the analog input problem. Using principles of stochastic logic, we construct a digital tunability circuit that only requires an approximately 50/50 random bitstream from stochastic nan-odevices such as magnetic tunnel junctions, electronic, pho-tonic diodes or other nanodevices with intrinsic entropy. We experimentally demonstrate with stochastic magnetic tunnel junction devices (sMTJ) that this approach yields sigmoidal tuning curves without any analog overhead. Moreover, we show how sMTJ device variations including relaxation time and bias offsets can be mitigated through calibrated delay lines and XOR whitening. Building on this tunability, we enable two key capabilities. First, the asynchronous operation enables self-coloring and random-order updates. The intrinsic timing variations of the sMTJs naturally resolve conflicts without explicit graph coloring, which supports scalable parallelism. Second, the tunability circuit enables on-chip annealing by simply changing delay lines, demonstrated by a sampling experiment with five sMTJs. Notably, on-chip annealing has been a limitation of all prior digital p-bit designs where new weights have to be shuttled between the chip and off-chip memory or moved within on-chip memory, any time the computational temperature is decreased. Finally, we benchmark our design using a 7 nm standard cell flow and demonstrate significant reductions in area and power compared to alternative DAC-free p-bit architectures (digital and mixed signal). Our work establishes a robust next step towards scalable probabilistic computing in CMOS + X systems.

While gain cell memory decouples the read and write paths, its storage mechanism remains coupled with read and write. This work presents the design and experimental demonstration of a 3D-scalable amplified memory (Amp Cell) featuring complete decoupling of read, write, and store: Si FET for read, oxide semiconductor FET for write, and compact MIM capacitor for store. Through systematic device-array-circuit co-design, the Amp Cell requires no additional process steps, control signals, or significant circuit modifications compared to gain cell memory, while improving retention by >5× at VDD = 1 V and enabling both self-refresh capability and 3D scalability. The nT(nC) Amp Cell establishes a continuum bridging 1T1C DRAM and 2T gain cell, combining the advantages of both while eliminating their fundamental limitations.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353618 | Gain Cell Memory Scalability to 5-nm and Beyond
Memory architectures fundamentally comprise not only individual storage cells but complete systems integrating memory arrays with their essential peripheral circuits, which are equally critical for overall performance. This work examines gain cell memory array architecture and peripheral circuit design using fabricated N40 chip measurements and N5 node simulations. 3× density of HD SRAM was achieved through innovative folded and interleaved array layouts combined with 3D-stacked cell structures. The optimized 256×256 hybrid gain cell macro operates at 2 GHz array write/read frequency (95% of HD SRAM) with a cell size of 0.007 μm2 at the 5-nm node. Importantly, we developed a comprehensive high-level array model for gain cell assessment and optimization, introducing the device metrics Qg×REFF that incorporates array-level effects to provide systematic guidance for gain cell device design.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353800 | 5nm FinFET Differential Plasma Recorder Device
A newly developed FinFET-based wafer-level differential plasma charge recorder fabricated using a 5nm FinFET CMOS logic process is first presented in this paper. DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353734 | Mechanics of Integration and Component Performance Step-up for Nanosheet Transistors with Ultrathin 2D Channel
Mechanical strength is a key parameter in nanosheet (NS) integration especially for ultra-thin channels such as 2D materials. MoS2 bilayer enables NS channel integrity at longer gate lengths as well as C-contact geometry with an effective contact length (Leff) of up to 30 nm. To enable contact integration, nearly epitaxial metal is deposited by atomic layer deposition (ALD). The extracted work function is ~6.0 eV in contact with WSe2 making it a good candidate for PMOS contact. Combined with Phosphorous doping, all elements for fab-compatible contact module are included here. Just like in 3D semiconductors, channel mobility can be limited by the gate dielectrics. Here we show ~1.6x mobility enhancement by introducing an appropriate IL and surface treatment compared to our previous report [1]. The purpose of the IL is to reduce remote phonon scattering from the dielectric while the surface preparation reduces interface defects. DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353681 | Bandgap-Engineered Boron Oxynitride Tunneling Layer for Reliable PLC operation of 3D V-NAND Flash Memory Devices
We introduce Boron Oxynitride (BON) as a novel tunneling layer material which can replace SiON in the conventional SiO2/SiON/SiO2 tunneling layer stack. Compared to SiON, BON exhibits a smaller valence band offset and a larger conduction band offset with respect to silicon. These properties enhance hole injection during erase operation and reduce electron loss during retention. These characteristics of BON help overcome the tradeoff between erase efficiency and the reliability of flash memory. A memory device with a BON tunneling layer stack shows 25.4% steeper ISPE slope, 9 times faster erase before cycling, and 23 times faster erase after cycling, compared to a device with a SiON tunneling layer stack. Furthermore, BON significantly reduces trap generation, resulting in superior retention, endurance, and narrower cell-to-cell variation. Consequently, it successfully enables reliable Penta-Level-Cell (PLC) operation, achieving a 3 times lower standard deviation and a 4 times larger state-to-state margin compared to the control device with SiON tunneling layer.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353477 | Experimental demonstration of robust high temperature operation with 104 endurance and stable VTh at 125 °C enabled by IL scavenging in Si Channel FEFETs
Endurance degradation in conventional Si-Channel ferroelectric FETs (FEFETs) is predominantly governed by trap generation within the interfacial SiO₂ (IL) layer, where elevated electric fields accelerate defect generation, leading to polarization screening and progressive memory window (MW) collapse. In this work, we demonstrate that using scavenging to thin the IL layer induces a sub-stoichiometric SiOx, with higher capacitance which dramatically improves the endurance characteristics of the FEFET. This IL engineering results in a redistribution of voltage and a new mode of FEFET failure driven by the underlying FE rather than the IL. This FE-driven degradation paradigm yields three critical advantages: (1) suppression of subthreshold slope (SS) deterioration by minimizing interface state buildup, (2) MW stability over extended cycling, as evidenced by a stable high-VTh and low-VTh states until FE breakdown, and (3) reduction in read-after-write latency due to faster de-trapping of traps in IL layer. We also demonstrate robust MW at 125°C with 104 cycles endurance and low SS. Further, we quantify trap generation and electric fields in the IL and FE layers using Ginestra™ simulations. These findings establish an innovative and efficient pathway to high endurance, stable VTh FEFETs via the implementation of an IL engineering-driven control of ferroelectric degradation.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353675 | Low VPGM Operation, Read Disturb, and Cell-to-Cell Interference in Scaled Vertical FeNAND

This marks a 4.4 V reduction compared to previous results, while achieving record retention and endurance, setting a new benchmark in FeNAND performance. We present the first experimental evaluation of read disturb (RD) and cell-to-cell interference (C2C) in FeNAND at scaled word line (WL) pitch, with direct benchmarking against charge trap NAND. The observed increases in RD (3×) and C2C (7×) are attributed to design trade-offs made to achieve the wider memory window, including thinner channel interlayers and gate-side charge injection operation. TCAD simulations reveal that electric field crowding at WL corners induces localized dipole flipping, intensifying C2C interference. These challenges can be mitigated through advanced gate stack engineering, optimized geometries as demonstrated in simulation, and alternative biasing schemes. These findings underscore critical design considerations for enabling scalable, low-voltage FeNAND integration in future 3D NAND architectures.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353700 | Towards a Low-Disorder pFET Gate Stack for Monolayer WSe2 Channels

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353770 | Direct Observation of Nanoscale Polarization Switching in HZO Ferroelectrics Using STEM-EBIC
We report the experimental observation of discrete ferroelectric switching down to 0.475 nm × 0.475 nm in HfO2-based ferroelectrics using correlated STEM electron beam induced current (EBIC) imaging and nanoscale voltage pulsing. These high-resolution measurements reveal single-domain switching events characterized by abrupt, bi-state transitions with no intermediate polarization states. By mapping polarization–voltage behavior and switching kinetics across thousands of domains, we extract spatially resolved switching thresholds (Emin), switching speeds (tsw), and remanent polarization (2Pr), uncovering robust scaling trends. Larger domains exhibit nearly twice the polarization and faster switching despite higher Emin, while smaller domains switch more slowly at the same field. Sub-domain imaging enables quantification of variation scaling: polarization variation increases linearly logarithmically with area, with inter-domain variation dominating intra-domain variation. Importantly, we show that multi-bit operation arises from the statistical distribution of domain-level switching—rather than partial polarization—enabling distinct and reliable states in devices as small as 0.01 μm2. These findings provide a foundational experimental framework for designing scalable, multibit ferroelectric memory based on grain- and domain-level heterogeneity.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353585 | Experiments and Modeling of Defect Dynamics and BTI in Doped In2O3 TFTs Undergoing Densification during 400 °C Post-BEOL Forming Gas Annealing (FGA)
We report, for the first time, the impact of essential, process-driven 400 °C hydrogen (H)-based post-BEOL annealing (i.e., FGA) on the electrical characteristics and BTI behavior of oxide channel TFTs, along with the associated morphological and defect evolution, through a combined experimental study and DFT-based liquid-quench molecular dynamics (MD) simulations. As a model system, we choose 3.7 nm thick 4 % W-doped In2O3-channel TFTs with a 5 nm plasma-ALD-grown HfO2 gate oxide, encapsulated by a 3 nm Al2O3 / 3 nm HfO2 hybrid layer that effectively blocks incoming hydrogen. The key findings are: (1) Early-stage densification during FGA (10 min) generates shallow- /delocalized In–H–In defect states (~0.65 eV below Fermi-level), causing PBTI degradation (from -27.45 mV in the pristine state to −122 mV under 2 ks stressing at 85 °C, Vov=+1.5V) and introducing a distinct IDS–VGS "kink"; (2) Prolonged annealing (>40 min) triggers a recovery due to partial crystallization, trapping pre-existing H in deep/localized defect states (In–OH-induced In–In), limiting H mobility, restoring low PBTI (−10.4 mV) and erasing the "Kink"; (3) Overall, a 60 min FGA steepens SS by ~37%, improves both PBTI and NBTI with an associated sign change. These results elucidate H-defect dynamics during critical post-BEOL FGA and underscore the importance of hydrogen-blocking encapsulations for BEOL integration of oxide-channel TFTs without sacrificing performance and reliability.

Key technical advancements include: i) Novel dual-gate engineering of nvCAP, effectively addressing the weak erase and poor retention challenges with oxide-channel ferroelectrics; ii) First successful BEOL integration of DG nvCAP on a foundry CMOS chip, achieving a non-destructive capacitive on/off ratio of ~64.4 at Vread=0 V—a record among reported BEOL-compatible nvCAPs; iii) Non-destructive read endurance exceeding 109 cycles at |Vread|=1 V, achieved in BEOL-integrated DG nvCAPs; iv) Introduction of the M3D non-destructive "capacitive" digital-compute-in-memory (Cap-DCIM) paradigm, achieving 146× higher figure-of-merit (TOPS/W×TOPS/mm2) compared to leading analog CIMs and 111× lower static power versus SRAM-based DCIMs—enabled by highly scalable digital logic and BEOL integration of DG nvCAPs. Finally, we experimentally validate the operational principle of M3D Cap-DCIM, by demonstrating BEOL capacitance-modulated FEOL Si transistor current amplification through monolithically integrated DG nvCAP on a foundry CMOS chip. These advancements offer a promising pathway to overcome the long-standing scalability and variability limitations of leading analog CIM approaches.

DOI 10.1109/IEDM50572.2025.11353773 | Sub-micron AlN Films with High Thermal Conductivity and Electrical Resistivity for Efficient Heat Dissipation in 3D ICs
Poly-AlN films are explored as a promising scalable thermal material for 3D ICs. Sub-500 nm thick poly-AlN films, PVD sputter-deposited below 200°C on various substrates, achieve exceptional cross-plane and in-plane thermal conductivities up to 100 Wm⁻1K⁻1 and 30 Wm⁻1K⁻1, respectively. DOI 10.1109/IMW61990.2025.11026973 | Comparative Study of Channel Materials for Ferroelectric NAND Applications
In this work, we compare the memory window (MW) and retention performance of laminated gate stacks for ferroelectric NAND (Fe-NAND) applications, considering both polysilicon and amorphous oxide semiconductor (AOS) channels. The laminated gate stack consists of a 3 nm Al2O3 layer sandwiched between two 8 nm Hafnium Zirconium oxide (HZO) layers. Our results show that AOS channel-based ferroelectric field-effect transistors (Fe-FETs) exhibit a 1.5× larger MW than their polysilicon counterparts at comparable write voltage, primarily due to the absence of a channel interfacial layer (IL) in AOS devices. However, polysilicon Fe-FETs demonstrate significantly better retention, with a retention loss of less than 0.1% at 1000 seconds. In contrast, AOS-based Fe-FETs experience a 37% retention loss at 1000 seconds. We hypothesize that this is due to the presence of oxygen vacancies (Vo2+) in the channel, which deplete the electrons over time and increases the depolarization field to cause MW degradation. This study underscores the critical role of channel material selection in optimizing MW and retention performance for Fe-NAND applications.

DOI 10.1109/IMW61990.2025.11026956 | Modeling of the impact of elliptical shapes on main Read Window Budget mechanisms in 3D NAND
Accurately predicting the Read-Window-Budget (RWB) of a NAND array with complex-shaped cell transistors using TCAD is challenging due to limitations in handling the distribution of the entire array. In this study, we propose a novel method that integrates TCAD results with in-line physical characterization to investigate the impact of pillar shape on VT distribution, primarily at the block level. Additionally, we introduce a method to derive degradation mechanisms using large and small circle parallel circuit paradigm. The effectiveness of these approaches is validated by comparing the Read Disturb predicted by simulations with the shape dependence observed in Si data. By confirming the shape dependence of the main RWB degradation components, including Read Disturb, Program Disturb, Cell-to-Cell Interference, we demonstrate the feasibility of predicting RWB degradation by means of identified degradation mechanisms and a straightforward approach even for cell transistors with complex shapes.

DOI 10.1109/IMW68301.2026.11532844 | Experimental Decomposition of Threshold Voltage Distribution in 176-Layer Tlc Vertical Nand Flash Memory
We investigate the threshold voltage $(\boldsymbol{V}_{\text{th }}$) distribution of a 176-layer TLC vertical NAND (VNAND) flash memory by experimentally decomposing it into physically distinct components. The measured distribution is separated into an ideal baseline defined by programming conditions, intrinsic broadening evaluated at the page-level, and interaction-induced contributions emerging at the block-level. Intrinsic variation is dominated by read variation including random telegraph noise (RTN), while initial $V_{\text{th }}$ shift (IVS) also contributes to distribution degradation. Among interaction effects, word-line (WL) coupling induces width broadening, whereas other mechanisms primarily cause $\boldsymbol{V}_{\text{th }}$ shift without distribution widening. This decomposition clarifies which degradation components are practically improvable and which are fundamentally constrained by device architecture and operation.

DOI 10.1109/IPFA65338.2025.11256741 | The Innovative Method for Analyzing Package Protectability with Ball-on-Ring and Ball-on-Plate

The inception of this research begins with the issue of the substrate (SBT) found dent cases that have the failure mode showing the BE (backend) package electrical test failure. The failure analysis shows the package has a dent on SBT and no package crack. However, the chip, which is presumed to be protected by the packaging, and the bottom die already exhibited a crack situation that resulted in the electrical failure. This means that the die crack is more sensitive to external force than the package crack, and the normal package strength test which tests the package and shows a crack, would not be suitable for simulating the real SBT dent failure cases, since the die will break prior to the package breaks although the dies are protected by package molding and SBT. Therefore, to simulate and check how the die is protected by the package rather than just check the package strength, a new method will be needed. To simulate the real failure mode, Ball-on-Ring (BOR) and Ball-on-Plate (BOP) tests were adopted in this work for inducing different stress types and be capable of doing the electrical test. Failure analysis and virtual simulation were applied to validate BOR and BOP to see which was close to the real failure and selected as the main evaluation method. In this study, an innovative method that combined physical and electrical tests was performed to investigate the package protectability to die based on the BOR and BOP methods. This novel method will help provide a quantitative result for estimation and assess the correlated issues on similar package products in the future.

DOI 10.1109/IPFA65338.2025.11256754 | PVC Sensitivity Recovery from De-Trapping Charges by Accessible Thermal Annealing in Sub-4nm
Passive Voltage Contrast (PVC) is an electron beam-based technique for detecting nanoscale defects by mapping the surface voltage distribution of semiconductor devices. Notably, as device scaling continues to shrink, the size of defects also decreases, and the utility of PVC becomes increasingly prominent. However, the sensitivity of PVC significantly diminishes following high electron beam irradiation due to the presence of interface charge traps. Various methods have been investigated to mitigate the presence of interface charge traps. However, these techniques typically involve demanding conditions, such as exceptionally high temperatures and pressures in specific atmospheres, which are primarily aimed at enhancing transistor performance in a fab. In the context of physical failure analysis, it is important to note that many metal materials exhibit vulnerability at elevated temperatures, and the original configuration of defects can be altered. Therefore, it is crucial to develop charge trap release techniques that minimize physical damage while effectively addressing the issue. In this study, we investigated a method to recover the sensitivity of PVC through annealing. The effectiveness of this method was validated using scanning electron microscopy (SEM), transmission electron microscopy (TEM), and nano-probing techniques. Based on the experimental results, we optimized the annealing conditions according to the specific surface metals, resulting in an improved PVC sensitivity that surpassed the levels observed prior to high electron beam irradiation.

In this paper, we report on the reliability impacts of tapered-nanosheets (NSs) and metal-gate-boundary (MGB) localized layout effects (LLE) in Multi-Bridge-Channel field effect transistors (MBCFETs) fabricated using 2nm gate-allaround (GAA) logic technology. We subjected T-shaped, dumbbell-shaped (D-shape), and stair-shaped (S-shape) structures to bias temperature instability (BTI) tests. Unique transitions in tapered NSs alter the source/drain (SD) volume, leading to stress changes in the channel. N-type transistors (SGN) positive BTI (PBTI) threshold voltage (${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$) shifts are primarily influenced by initial threshold voltage (${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}0}}$), while P-type transistors (SGP) negative BTI (NBTI) ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$ shifts are affected by both of ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}0}}$ and stress induced hole trap barrier changes. Additionally, SGP NBTI ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$ shifts exhibit different behaviors depending on the width ratio. The BTI behavior in fall-type and rise-type S-shaped structures is similar to that in T-shaped and D-shaped structures. BTI ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$ shifts by MGB distance are influenced by oxygen vacancy exchange-induced ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}0}}$ shifts. Furthermore, BTI ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$ shifts due to neighboring ${{\mathrm{V}}_{\mathrm{t}}}$ options are modified by dipole formation.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982963 | The Voltage Dependence of NBTI: Resolution of Controversy Surrounding Power Law vs. Exponential
Negative Bias Temperature Instability is typically measured at high stress voltages and then projected to the lower voltage use conditions using a model with either power law or exponential voltage dependence. The choice of the right model is crucial, as it can lead to vastly different estimations of degradation at use conditions. In this work, using a variety of characterization methods, we demonstrate that the NBTI can show an apparent power law voltage dependence during the early stage of degradation, but an exponential voltage dependence model is needed for accurate use-condition projections. We also demonstrate that empirical extrapolation from conventional NBTI measurements, without proper consideration of the recovery effect, may lead to a perceived power law dependence. Finally, using months-long NBTI characterization on various gate stacks and device architectures, we confirm the generality of the behavior that the power law voltage model can significantly underestimate the degradation close to product use conditions.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982937 | Comprehensive Study of Non-Conducting Stress Characteristics in Ultra-Scaled FinFET
In this work, a comprehensive study of device characteristics shift induced by non-conducting stress (NCS) is presented. Additionally, the unique recovery behavior of NCS is revealed in detail by silicon data. Through experiments, the mechanism of NCS can be separated to three parts: (1) permanent interface traps caused by impact ionization, (2) recoverable HK/IL traps and (3) recoverable oxide charge by band-to-band-tunneling. Also, geometry plays an important role for NCS performance. A longer gate length shows a better NCS performance. Finally, we verify that the optimizing quality of interface between drain and gate can result in a better NCS performance. Furthermore, the device characteristics shifts by NCS can be compensated by other reliability items in the real circuit operation.

In this paper we present a review of the BAT framework to model BTI degradation of DRAM CMOS Periphery N and P FETs. DC and AC measurements are performed using ultra-fast measurement methodology. In the first part, the focus is on DC PBTI and NBTI degradation. For both N and P FETs, two gate oxide thicknesses are evaluated and then compared to the model to evaluate the capabilities of the BAT framework. In the second part, the modeling effort is then shifted toward AC BTI degradation. The duty factor and frequency are investigated. Finally, in the last part a discussion on the BAT modeling performances is shared.

PBTI dVth decreased by 43% with decreasing PBTI duty. Especially, we found that the recovery effect of 80% of IGZO was much larger than 25% of Si because its shallow trap and acceleration effect of Vpp.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983902 | Record-Low EOT(3.6Å) & Jleak(7×10-8A/cm2@0.8V) HZO by Dielectric-Selective Microwave Annealing

The MWA supplies the energy required for HZO crystallization in both thermal and microwave forms, offering three key benefits: (i) It drastically reduces thermal budget (TB), suppresses interfacial dead layer formation, and enhances endurance ($\mathrm{K}\sim 62$ at 1012 cycles). (ii) It only affects the HZO film and not the TiN electrodes, making the dipoles inside HZO vibrate. This enables selective and volumetric annealing in high-aspect-ratio DRAM cell capacitors. (iii) It lowers the activation barrier for HZO crystallization compared to typical rapid thermal annealing (RTA). We experimentally verified this by quantifying activation energy (Ea) using electrical measurements under various TBs, finding that the Ea was 0.67 eV/f.u. for MWA, and 0.85 eV/f.u. for RTA.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982749 | Enhanced Memory Performance in Ferroelectric NAND Applications: The Role of Tunnel Dielectric Position for Robust 10-Year Retention
We report a comprehensive study on memory window (MW) enhancement and retention loss (RL) in ferroelectric (FE) field effect transistors for NAND applications with a dielectric layer inserted within the FE film (next to the gate ‒ GBL devices ‒ or in the middle of the FE layer ‒ TDL devices). Using a temperature-dependent modeling framework, we show that MW and RL properties are controlled respectively by carriers trapping into defects at the dielectric-FE interface and by its fragile balance with FE depolarization. While MW enhancement is achieved whenever the inserted dielectric layer is present, a robust retention is obtained only in TDL FEFETs, thanks to a stabilization of the trapped charge preventing FE depolarization. Simulation results clearly demonstrate that the fragile balance between above phenomena is ultimately controlled by the position of the dielectric layer, which determines i) the field redistribution within the stack, ii) the magnitude of charge de-trapping, and iii) the magnitude of the depolarization field. Our results provide critical guidelines for the improvement of FE 3D-NAND reliability.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983406 | Investigating Read-After-Write Delay in Ferroelectric FET with Gate-Side Injection
The conventional HfO2-based n-type ferroelectric field-effect transistors (nFeFETs) suffer from read-after-write delay (RAWD), caused by electron detrapping at the channel interface after positive write pulses. In this work, we propose a metal-insulator-ferro-insulator-silicon (MIFIS) nFeFET structure incorporating a SiNx charge trapping layer between the ferroelectric and gate metal to introduce gate-side injection (GSI) of positive charges under positive write pulses. This mechanism reinforces the remnant polarization and the interlayer electric field to prevent channel-side electron detrapping, leading to enhanced memory window and immediate read-after-write capability. Experimental results demonstrate that the MIFIS FeFETs with 5nn-thick SiNx charge trapping layer exhibit significantly larger MW of 7.9V and negligible low-threshold voltage (LVT) shifts between µs and 1s after write operation delay compared to conventional metal-ferro-insulator-silicon (MFIS) FeFETs. Furthermore, TCAD simulations on MIFIS nFeFETs with 5nm SiNx and 10nm ferroelectric layer reveal the underlying mechanisms, showing that the additional injected charges strengthen polarization and create an energy barrier against electron detrapping. These findings highlight the potential of MIFIS structures in overcoming RAWD issue, enabling faster operation in FeFET-based memory systems.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983050 | Impact of Mechanical Stress on IGZO TFTs: Enhancing PBTI Degradation
This study investigates the impact of out-of-plane compressive mechanical stress (MS) on the performance and reliability of n-channel IGZO thin film transistors (TFTs). DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983636 | Comprehensive Study of Saturation Current Impact on LDMOS Reliability

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983241 | New Burnout Failure at the Chip Edge: Analysis and Preventive Design by a Novel Experimental Approach
To meet the growing demand for high bit density, memory devices have adopted 3-D stacking technology. In many applications, vertical packaging height is constrained, so as the stacked memory cells within the chip increase in thickness, the silicon substrate thickness must decrease. When a thinner substrate with modified chip warpage is combined with a triboelectric charging during the packaging process, it leads to a newly observed burnout failure at the chip edge, which manifests as standby failures in DC tests after assembly. This failure mode causes significant yield issues in multi-die packages and also threatens product reliability. In this study, we identify the root cause through failure analysis and isolate the dominant factors through electrical and thermal-mechanical analyses. Finally, we propose a process design to mitigate these burnout failures.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983375 | Novel Trigger Circuit & SCR Device Co-Engineering Based Local (I/O-VSS & I/O-VDD) ESD Clamp Concepts with Improved Latch-Up Susceptibility, Lower Leakage and Lower Capacitance for Ultra High Speed I/Os
In this work, a novel silicon-controlled rectifier (SCR) based local protection concept is presented with co-engineered device and trigger circuit. This results in improved latch-up robustness, lower PAD capacitive loading and lower leakage when compared to the reference (state-of-the-art till date) concept. The proposed and reference concepts are realized in Samsung 28nm process for experimental validation and benchmarking. Its TLP/vf-TLP characteristics and other ESD measurement results along with the design trade-offs are presented and compared with the reference concept. A TCAD based device and circuit co-optimization (DTCO) methodology is also presented. The proposed concept can realize the demanding I/O needs of SCR based local clamps, qualified for HBM to CDM time domains, and meeting ESD specs required for high speed I/O buffers.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982926 | Well-Charging Damage to Capacitors Connected Between VDD and VSS in a Single Power Domain
In this paper, we report on the charging damage observed in capacitors situated within a single Deep N-Well (DNW) during integrated circuit processing. These capacitors, connected between VDD and VSS within a single power domain, accumulate charge from backend metal/via antennas during plasma etching. Our study delves into the behavior and mechanisms underlying this charging damage, utilizing extensive test-patterns. Based on the results and the identified mechanisms, we propose the layout and design guidelines to mitigate such damage.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982917 | Electromigration Performance of Cu Bumps in Various Configurations
To investigate the effect of bump structure on electromigration (EM) performance, four bump structures were designed: Cu/solder/OSP, Cu/Ni/solder/OSP, Cu/solder/ENEPIG, and Cu/Ni/solder/ENEPIG. The EM test results show that the EM performance of Cu bumps is significantly affected by the bump scheme and the direction of electron flow. The early failure rate is higher when the electron flow originates from the Ni-rich barrier side. Furthermore, the Cu/Ni/solder/ENEPIG system shows the highest early failure rate due to its single Sn grain structure. This study challenges the previous belief regarding the Ni barrier, suggesting that the inclusion of a Ni-rich barrier may necessarily have a detrimental impact on the EM performance of Cu bumps.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10982822 | The Opportunity of Anti-ferroelectrics in FeFET for Emerging Non-Volatile Memory Applications
We demonstrate a novel Ferroelectric FET (FeFET) configuration designed to expand memory window (MW) and enhance retention via two approaches: (i) the introduction of a charge trap layer (CTL) and (ii). the adoption of anti-ferroelectric (AFE) material. The retention loss in metal-interlayer (IL)-ferroelectric (FE)-IL-silicon (MIFIS) FeFET is ascribed to the negative feedback loop, involving the de-trap of interface trap charge from gate (Qit‘) and the loss of polarization (P) which mutually accelerates the further loss of the other. Our first approach, CTL on top of FE, provides additional trap sites with deep trap levels which introduces favorable charge (QN) with high stability, thus enhancing both MW and retention. Moreover, by utilizing the high spontaneous polarization (Ps) and low remanent polarization (Pr) nature of AFE, we further improved the retention characteristics by increasing the proportion of QN and decoupling the negative feedback loop. By proposing a new FeFET design with a high MW (~12.9 V), a low operation voltage (+17/-14 V), and enhanced data retention, we suggest that the hafnia-based FeFET can be a big step forward for 3D NAND technology.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS48204.2025.10983009 | Understanding of Incremental Step Pulse Programming (ISPP) Slope Degradation in 3D NAND and its Band-Engineered Trap Layer Solution
We propose a physical model to understand the degradation factors of incremental step pulse programming (ISPP) slope, a critical efficiency indicator for program operation in charge trap flash (CTF)-based 3D NAND Flash memory. During ISPP operation, the position of the trapped charges in the charge trap layer (CTL) significantly affects the channel surface potential, which in turn influences $\boldsymbol{V}_{\text{Th}}$. In this work, for the first time, we developed an ISPP slope model to capture the sensitivity of the channel surface potential to the position and density of charge trapped within the CTL. Our findings indicate that a major cause of ISPP slope degradation is the non-uniform trapped charge distribution within the CTL, in contrast to the uniform distribution in floating gate (FG) memory under thermal equilibrium state. Using the derived model, we successfully reproduce experimental ISPP slope and propose a band-engineered CTL to improve the ISPP slope by redistributing trapped charges.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499325 | Comprehensive Analysis of Hot Carrier Injection Reliability in FinFET LDMOS Devices with Various Trench Structures and Doping Configurations
In this study, hot carrier injection (HCI) degradation test and simulation were conducted for fin-based laterally diffused MOS (LDMOS) devices with varying process conditions. Trench structure, channel length, and doping profile significantly influence HCI degradation. In particular, the interface doping profile in the drift region induces HCI degradation trends that differ from those observed in CMOS devices. Since these device process characteristics have a critical impact on both reliability and performance, careful device design that considers both performance and reliability under various process conditions is essential to provide optimal high-performance computing capabilities. Finally, an on/off HCI degradation model was provided to predict fin LDMOS HCI degradation under circuit operating conditions.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499315 | Comprehensive Study of Single Event Upset in TSMC N3E Logic Technology
This work presents a comprehensive study on single event upset (SEU) in the TSMC N3E logic technology process, utilizing an in-house test chip to investigate the impacts of applied voltage, threshold voltage options, hardened circuit designs, deep-N-Well integration, and test facility beam quality. Our findings reveal that applied voltage and threshold voltage options can be synergistically combined into a novel metric, “Voltage Headroom,” which significantly improves the predictability of failure rates based on specific application requirements, and tests with quasimonoenergetic neutrons and protons demonstrated comparable error rates, thereby allowing users to choose facilities based on availability. Based on these insights, we provide specific guidelines for TSMC N3E users targeting data center applications, recommending lower threshold voltage options with higher applied voltages, highlighting that deep-N-Well designs significantly enhance SEU robustness in low voltage headroom applications, and emphasizing that hardened circuit designs remarkably improve robustness by over two orders of magnitude.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499350 | Analysis of the Width- and Area- Dependence Discrepancy in the GAA Structure TDDB using Numerical Simulation
The effects and causes of the size of nanosheet width on time-dependent-dielectric-breakdown (TDDB) were analyzed through numerical simulations, focusing on the NMOS operation of gate-all-around (GAA) devices. We solved the Schrodinger-Poisson equation in a fully coupled manner, and using the resulted $\vert\vec{E}\vert$-field, conducted a TDDB simulation by generating random traps according to the thermochemical trap generation model and monitored whether percolation paths are formed. As a result, the Weibull distributions of time-to-breakdown (TB D) obtained from approximately 100 devices for each nanosheet width were identical despite the difference in width. This is because traps and percolation paths are concentrated in the oxide corner region by $\vert\vec{E}\vert$-field crowding, indicating that the TDDB of GAA depends on the area of the nanosheet corners, i.e., the number of nanosheets, rather than the total area of the nanosheet.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499289 | Die of Interest – Die Level Predictive Modeling to Reduce Reliability Defect Escape
Reliability is a critical quality indicator in semiconductor manufacturing, especially for automotive and high-performance computing products, where latent defects can lead to catastrophic failures. Accurately identifying potential outlier dies while minimizing false positives and optimizing cost efficiency remains a constant challenge. This paper introduces a novel two-stage methodology to address this issue. In the first stage, statistical analysis and machine learning techniques are applied to semiconductor inline data to identify and rank dies exhibiting abnormal patterns, referred to as Die of Interest (DoI). The second stage is envisioned to apply adaptive testing, targeted stress testing, and reliability screening to these outlier dies identified by the Die of Interest framework, thereby ensuring comprehensive evaluation and enhanced reliability. Experimental results demonstrate a 9-fold improvement in identifying outlier dies compared to baseline, highlighting the effectiveness of this approach. The proposed methodology offers a promising solution for achieving superior reliability in semiconductor manufacturing while enhancing efficiency and reducing costs.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499295 | High-Field Breakdown Mechanism Limiting Write Endurance in Ferroelectric FET for 3D NAND
Ferroelectric FETs (FEFETs) have emerged as promising candidates to overcome scaling challenges in 3D charge-trap-flash (CTF) NAND, enabling further scaling. However, their reliability, such as write endurance, remains insufficiently explored. This work identifies the mechanism limiting write endurance under the high-field regime in FE NAND. Most failed cells experience gate oxide breakdown (BD) during program (PRG). Thus, anode hole injection (AHI) during PRG is found to be the dominant cause of BD. While FE NAND requires gate-side hole injection for achieving a sufficient memory window (MW), it is suggested that anode hot holes can damage the top insulator and eventually result in the gate stack BD, indicating a trade-off between the MW and write endurance.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499217 | Telemetry-Driven Time-Dependent Correction Factor Modeling for Shape Parameter Adjustment in Predictive DRAM Reliability
As demand for new data center deployments continues to grow, an increasing fraction of DRAM products are evaluated and operated during early deployment stages, including newly launched devices. Under these conditions, reliability assessment must often rely on limited early-life data, where short observation windows fundamentally hinder stable estimation of hazard behavior, particularly the Weibull shape parameter. Bias introduced at this stage propagates directly into long-term reliability projections. In this work, we show that long-term reliability prediction is fundamentally limited not by the choice of forecasting models, but by systematic bias in extrapolating the Weibull shape parameter from short-term observations, which persists across both statistical and machine-learning-based approaches. To address this limitation, we define a timedependent correction factor based on recurring temporal patterns of shape extrapolation bias consistently observed under similar early observation conditions across historical products. By incorporating this correction framework, long-term hazard behavior can be reasonably inferred from early telemetry alone, without reliance on long-horizon observations from the target system. Validation using large-scale field datasets shows that the proposed approach stabilizes shape trajectories, with failure-rate metrics emerging naturally as consistent secondary outcomes. The framework is broadly applicable to early-stage reliability assessment across field deployment, internal qualification, and customer evaluation scenarios.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499239 | A Comprehensive Study of Terrestrial Radiation Effects in Current Generation DRAM
Fast neutron, thermal neutron and alpha particle relative upset rates determined via accelerated measurements in multiple DRAM technology generations are presented. Technology scaling trends are explained via device simulations, and array to logic upset ratios are shown/explained. The threshold for total ionizing dose damage from terrestrial usecases is measured and shown to be inconsequential. Radiationinduced upsets in large DRAM deployments are extracted and shown to be a small fraction of other sources of upsets.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499313 | Barrier Integrity Evaluation Methods in Copper Interconnects
The electromigration (EM) immortality, exhibiting no sign of failure even after a prolonged duration of stress, can be attributed to the continuous supply of Cu from a wide underlying feeder through the via with poor barrier coverage. This type of barrier defect can artificially inflate the estimated EM lifetime, leading to a misleadingly optimistic reliability risk assessment in the lab. EM risk can also emerge in actual products due to the stringent design and aggressive scaling in advanced interconnects, especially with short-length EM rules. For EM risk mitigation, it is essential to have effective methods to reveal such barrier defects. This paper presents two PFA techniques for barrier integrity evaluation. The chemical etch method is very simple yet effective, and can be readily performed in general PFA labs. The STEM tomography requires an advanced TEM but can pinpoint the defect location.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499233 | Unified Numerical Modeling of Program Efficiency in 3D NAND Flash: AN Enabler for Design Optimization of Advanced 3D NAND Architectures
We present a structure-aware physical modeling framework for program efficiency analysis in advanced 3D NAND Flash architectures. DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499304 | Effects of Charge Confinement Under the Gate on Data Retention in 3D NAND Flash Memory
This study aims to characterize the impact of VPASS applied to adjacent word lines during program operation on longterm data retention loss. Experimental results demonstrate that VPASS drives charge loss rate from the charge trap layer: higher VPASS bias results in a lower charge loss. By applying a stretched exponential retention model, we show that VPASS modulates both the energy distribution of occupied traps, and the maximum threshold voltage shift by controlling the charge loss dynamics within the storage material. This was explained by TCAD simulations: higher VPASS leads to a lower density of charge, consequently leading to a lower electric field during data retention bake.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499311 | Analysis on SER failure mechanism and improvement by implementing RHBD in DRAM
Single event upset (SEU) caused by cosmic rays continuously occur throughout the device's lifetime and poses serious threats to the quality and reliability of dynamic random access memory (DRAM). The dominant mechanism of single event functional interrupt (SEFI) in DRAM was identified and verified through both simulation and neutron-irradiation experiment. SEFI is a misaddressed phenomenon caused by latch flip of redundant circuit in DRAM. In order to mitigate the vulnerability of the dual interlocked storage cell (DICE) latch, the charge sharing isolation (CSI) design was applied to the latest $12-\text{nm}$ process LPDDR5x (LP5x) product. After applying the CSI design, soft error rate (SER) improved by 61.8 % at the unit latch level and 75.6% at the product level compared with the previous generation. A SEFI-induced malfunction in DRAM does not cause cumulative damages, it can be recovered by initializing or rebooting the device. The newly introduced radiation hardened by design (RHBD) technique will be adopted in DRAM and will contribute to the improvement of DRAM reliability.

We report the first electrical detection methodology using gate-induced drain leakage (GIDL) asymmetry analysis—a paradigm-shifting breakthrough transforming reliability physics from reactive microscopy to proactive electrical monitoring. Our approach establishes quantitative correlations via band-to-band tunneling field perturbation analysis, enabling real-time monitoring with superior sensitivity. Through systematic optimization, we achieved zero-defect reliability, verified across over 20k total test evaluations, and established a comprehensive detection ecosystem, reducing turnaround-time by orders-of-magnitude. This work establishes the first electrical detection methodology for lateral silicide diffusion in advanced DRAM, providing a scalable foundation for next-generation reliability control.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499292 | Reliability Assurance Risks from BTI Recovery Effects
Circuit aging simulations face the challenge of correctly accounting for BTI recovery effects in the context of various AC and DC workloads. Many previous studies have shown the cyclical nature of BTI aging with the application of intermittent periods of DC stress. However, scant attention has been paid to the aging behavior for more realistic mission profiles comprised of alternating periods of AC and DC stress. Furthermore, applying the transistor-level understanding of aging behavior for realistic, complex waveforms to a practical simulation approach has not been well addressed in the literature, leading to possible underestimation of circuit-level aging. This work addresses this gap by quantifying transistor aging behavior under realistic conditions, then merges this understanding with circuit-level evaluations, and ultimately concludes that NBTI recovery benefits should not be universally assumed to apply to circuit aging reliability assessments.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499225 | Channel-Side Interlayer Engineering in Laminated Fe-FETs: Trap-Driven Optimization of Memory Window, Retention, and Disturb in Ferroelectric NAND Flash
In this work, we demonstrate for the first time that laminated Fe-FETs for Fe-NAND exhibit a distinct behavior: despite the common expectation that higher-$k$ channel-side interlayers (C-ILs) enhance reliability by lowering the equivalent oxide thickness (EOT), lower-$k$ C-ILs instead deliver superior memory-window (MW), retention, and pass-disturb performance. We systematically compare three different pure-oxide C-ILs $(\text{SiO}_{2}, \text{Al}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3}, \text{HfO}_{2})$ through experiments and TCAD modeling to elucidate trap-driven reliability. The results reveal that although high- $k$ C-IL layer reduce EOT, their higher trap densities cause MW narrowing, faster retention loss, and enhanced charge trapping. Devices with an $8 ~\text{nm} \text{Hf}_{0.5} \text{Zr}_{0.5} \mathrm{O}_{2} / 3 ~\text{nm} \text{Al}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3} / 8 ~\text{nm} \text{Hf}_{0.5} \text{Zr}_{0.5} \mathrm{O}_{2}$ stack and 1.5 nm C-ILs confirm that the low $k$ oxide achieves the most stable operation, exhibiting 15% larger MW, 1% retention loss at 1 ks, and 44% lower electron-trapping-induced disturb degradation after 106 passdisturb cycles. This behavior arises from trap-induced charges that outweighs the expected benefit of high dielectric constant, as confirmed by TCAD simulations. These findings highlight C-IL trap engineering as a key enabler for reliable, high-density FeNAND integration and clarify how channel IL-dependent traps govern reliability in laminated Fe-FETs.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499253 | Unraveling Intricate Switching Dynamics of FeFETs with Gate Side Injection Toward Reliable Disturb-Immune 3D NAND Storage
In this work, gate metal work function $(\Phi_{\mathrm{M}}$) engineering is employed to probe the switching dynamics in hafnium zirconium oxide (HZO)-based n-type ferroelectric field-effect transistors (FeFETs) with gate-side injection (GSI), targeting reliable 3D NAND storage. We show that switching behavior is governed by charge trapping during program/erase and post-pulse detrapping, both strongly modulated by the gate metal $\Phi_{\mathrm{M}}$. Four key observations are identified: (i) switching is jointly determined by charge trapping and subsequent detrapping; (ii) without detrapping, lower $\Phi_{\mathrm{M}}$ gates enhance GSI, resulting in larger threshold voltage $(\mathrm{V}_{\text{TH}}$) shifts or earlier switching onset at a given write voltage; (iii) after erase, lower $\Phi_{\mathrm{M}}$ gates experience increases GS electron detrapping during the delay, requiring higher write voltages to compensate charge loss; (iv) after programming, enhanced injection and polarization from the lower $\Phi_{\mathrm{M}}$ gates increase the electric field at the channel-side (CS) interlayer, suppressing CS electron detrapping. The results show that GSI FeFETs with evaporated aluminum gates exhibit higher onset voltages $(\mathrm{V}_{\text{ERS }}=-9 ~\mathrm{V}$ and $\mathrm{V}_{\text{PGM }}=10 ~\mathrm{V}$), higher DC breakdown voltages (-11.5 V), and superior disturb immunity $(\Delta \mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{T H}} \approx 0$ after 105 cycles at $\mathrm{V}_{\text{PASS }}=\mathbf{7 V}$).

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499257 | Characterization on Plasma Induced Damage in Thick Oxide Device at FinFET Technology
In this study, we investigated the plasma-induced damage (PID) characteristics of 14 nm FinFET with thick gate oxides, which are utilized in diverse applications such as highvoltage input/output (I/O), capacitors, and more. Test patterns were designed to examine various factors, including gate area, antenna ratio (the ratio of metal or via to gate area), antenna layer stack options, and the presence or absence of diodes. The experimental results of PID measurements for each metal layer and stack option revealed that plasma-induced damage in thick oxide devices tends to accumulate. Furthermore, we discovered that the allowable cumulative antenna ratio (cAR), which ensures safety from PID, decreases as the gate area increases, following a power-law dependence. Additionally, the experimental results for diodes showed significant differences in discharging efficiency depending on the junction type. The design guide derived from this study is expected to mitigate quality and reliability risks caused by PID and enhance circuit design efficiency.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499258 | Angle-Dependent Magnetic Field Immunity of 16nm Embedded STT-MRAM

Retention error measurements as a function of field angle are captured by a modified Néel-Brown model, from which the chip level maximum tolerable field $\left(H_{\max}\right)$ is extracted. In the active write mode, error rates show clear angular dependence–$\mathbf{P} \rightarrow \mathbf{A P}$ errors peak near 30° and $\mathbf{A P} \rightarrow \mathbf{P}$ errors near $\mathbf{9 0}^{{\circ}}$. This integrated experimental and simulation study provides predictive guidance for designing robust STTMRAM in magnetically noisy environments.

A physics-based Activated Barrier Double Well Thermionic (ABDWT) framework is validated on Long-Term retention loss in 176-, 232-, and 276- layer 3D TLC NAND Flash arrays under different program levels, patterns, number of Program/Erase cycles and bake temperatures.
