This is the first framework applicable across multiple 3D-NAND nodes with consistent parameters, surpassing empirical methods and enabling broader applicability, versatility and robustness in retention loss modeling for 3D NAND.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499301 | Fast In-Situ TDDB Prediction Method for HKMG Oxide Stacks
Conventional TDDB (Time-Dependent Dielectric Breakdown) testing using Constant Voltage Stress (CVS) provides accurate lifetime predictions but is time-intensive, requiring up to two days per wafer. This work proposes a fast, in-situ methodology combining Voltage Ramp Stress (VRS) and Fowler-Nordheim Delta Vt (FNDVt) measurements to estimate TDDB lifetime with high confidence and significantly reduced test time. The integrated suite enables 10 X throughput improvement while maintaining prediction accuracy within $1 \sigma$ of standard TDDB. Monte Carlo simulations are performed to consider the stochastic nature of the mechanism in the lifetime distributions. This approach has been validated across multiple process conditions.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499180 | Electromigration Performance and Underlying Mechanism in CoWoS Packaging
Electromigration (EM) has become one of the dominant reliability concerns in advanced packaging using interposers. DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499337 | Ultra-Fast TLP Characterization of Gate-All-Around (GAA) ESD Diodes for Low-Capacitance Optimized I/Os
This paper presents the characterization and modeling of FINFET fabricated diodes using bulk and gate-all-around (GAA) technologies, highlighting the enhanced electrostatic discharge (ESD) robustness observed in the GAAFINFET node. Ultra-fast Transmission Line Pulse (ufTLP) measurements with rise times of 40ps, and veryfast TLP (vf-TLP) measurements with rise times of 100ps were conducted, targeting both diode structures and gate oxide (GOX) victims. Diodes using nonplanar FINs with bulk node exhibited slower response and higher forward recovery (FR) overshoot, whereas GAA diodes demonstrated faster switching behavior without FR overshoot. GOX structures in the bulk node failed prematurely due to FR-induced stress, in contrast to the more robust performance observed with GAA diodes. The overshoot-free switching of ESD diodes processed in GAA-FINFET Intel technology allows for improvement of several key performance indicators (KPI) in the High Speed (HS) interfaces, as shown on the example of DDR.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499201 | Transistor Reliability in 18A RibbonFET Technology

In this work we discuss the reliability of RibbonFET transistors with comparisons of Bias Temperature Instability (BTI), Hot Carrier (HC) and Gate Oxide (GOX) Breakdown. It is shown that PowerVia has no negative impact on transistor reliability. DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499171 | Aging Characterization on Band Gap Reference Circuit
Band gap reference (BGR) circuits are crucial for maintaining a stable reference voltage under power supply and/or temperature fluctuation. However, limited studies are available on how aging will affect the performance of BGRs. In this study, we present an accelerated aging evaluation on a sub 1 V BGR circuit designed and fabricated in Intel 4 process technology, and validated on the Quality and Reliability On-Chip (QROC) platform. The BGR reference output voltage ($\mathrm{V}_{\text{ref }}$) of $\mathbf{6 3 6 ~ m V}$ was measured while sweeping the power supply voltage ($\mathrm{V}_{\text{cc}}$) from 1 V to 1.4 V across temperatures from $-20^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ to 120° C. The variation of $V_{\text{ref }}$ was $\pm 6 \text{mV}$, which ensured the robustness of the BGR circuit. The accelerated aging stress conditions were $\mathrm{V}_{\text{cc}}=1.45 \mathrm{V}$ and $\mathbf{T}=120^{\circ} \mathbf{C}$ for 8 days, corresponding to an estimated $\mathbf{1 0}$-year equivalent aging. After stress, $\mathbf{V}_{\text{ref }}$ degraded by approximately 1%, confirming BGR's robustness and precision, and highlighting the importance of aging evaluation for integrated-circuit (IC) performance and reliability.

DOI 10.1109/IRPS61424.2026.11499358 | Poly-Si Channel-Induced Time Instability of 3D NAND Flash Cell Threshold-Voltage: A Comprehensive Temperature Analysis
In this work, we investigate the time instability of the threshold voltage ($V_{T}$) of 3D nand Flash cells arising from the polycrystalline nature of their Si channel over an extremely wide temperature ($T$) range going from room temperature (RT) down to 4.2 K. Experimental data reveal that the magnitude of cell $V_{T}$ instability, first, increases from RT down to about 150 K and, then, decreases below the latter $T$. Through TCAD simulations, this behavior is explained as the outcome of the change with $T$ of the number of charge carriers trapped at the grain boundaries (GBs) involved in the phenomenon and of their impact on $V_{T}$. As a result of the observed turnaround, poly-Si channel-induced $V_{T}$ instability does not trouble the reliability of 3D nand Flash cells in the cryogenic regime more than at RT.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0552 | A Novel Approach to Multivariate Time-Series Anomaly Detection and Correlation Analysis for In-Fab Metrology Data

However, detecting abnormal phenomena in fabrication data remains a challenge due to the unique complexities of the semiconductor manufacturing process, which is characterized by numerous steps and interacting multivariate factors. This paper introduces a novel model for anomaly detection for time-series data based on 150 days of in-fab metrology data. The model reflects individual trends and data dispersions across all steps through the integration of statistical techniques. Specifically, the model performs a significance test-based correlation analysis among related tracking data from fab, including Process Program Identifications (PPIDs), equipment and chamber information, layout data and more. As a result, the proposed anomaly detection model has been successfully developed to be suitable for stages of high process variability. This model allows full detectability even for inspection items without predefined specifications and assigns a ranking based on the severity of anomalies.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2026p0628 | Low-Temperature CVD Oxide Deposition for Photoresist Protection

 Accurate transmission electron microscopy (TEM) characterization of photoresist (PR) is limited by preparation-induced artifacts that distort native morphology and obscure failure mechanisms, leading to unreliable root-cause identification in advanced-node failure analysis. We introduce an approach that decouples PR characterization from these artifacts by applying an ultra-thin (~10–20 Å) conformal CVD oxide deposited near room temperature (~30 °C). The oxide stabilizes the PR throughout FIB lamella preparation and low-dose TEM imaging, suppressing deformation, charging, and shrinkage while preserving intrinsic morphology. We benchmarked this method against common protection strategies, including M-Bond, standard Pt and Ir coatings, and e-beam-assisted deposition. All produced pronounced PR deformation and profile distortion from preparation and beam interaction rather than the native structure. In contrast, the low-temperature oxide improved profile measurement accuracy by ~7% and achieved a TEM imaging success rate near 99%. By removing artifact-driven ambiguity, the method reveals intrinsic process-induced PR damage that conventional workflows can mask or misinterpret. In a production case with a 12.5% yield loss, the distortion-free evidence enabled accurate root cause identification, redirected process development, and reduced yield loss to 0.3%. This approach turns PR TEM characterization into a reliable basis for correlating nanoscale structure with process-induced failure mechanisms.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2026p0107 | Analytical Approach for Revealing Latent Intermittent Column Failures Associated with Bit-Line Sense Amplifier Vulnerability in DRAM

 Intermittent column failures in high-density DRAM are investigated to identify their underlying physical mechanism. Comprehensive electrical characterization revealed that failing DRAM chips consistently exhibit relatively low threshold voltages (Vt) in the bit-line sense amplifier transistors (BLSA Tr), which markedly increase their susceptibility to sub-threshold leakage currents. However, this vulnerability can remain hidden due to circuit-level bit-line compensation, making it difficult to detect and screen using conventional analysis methods. To expose the concealed mechanism, multi-factor analytical conditions are devised that deliberately minimize the bit-line differential voltage (ΔVbl) by combining elevated temperature, prolonged retention time, and a controlled delay in sensing interval (tDS ). Under these conditions, the failure behavior of the low-Vt group, previously indistinguishable from that of the reference group, becomes clearly separated. In particular, extending tDS demonstrates the progressive degradation of ΔVbl induced by leakage, ultimately leading to functional sensing failure. These results confirm that the proposed methodology effectively unmasks hidden column-failure mechanisms and provides a robust, product-level failure analysis framework for identifying root causes in advanced DRAM technologies.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0527 | Failure Analysis of A16 GAA Device with BSPDN Architecture Using Electron Beam Probing

In the e-beam workflow development, several challenges were encountered. The complexity of metal signals led to issues such as crosstalk, charging, and contamination buildup due to prolonged e-beam exposure. The solution is to take advantage of e-beam pulsing function. Appropriate data collection methods were developed for Electron Voltage Imaging (EVI) and Electron Voltage Probing (EVP). For example, A continuous-wave (CW) e-beam scan was used for phase mapping (Phase EVI) to highlight the actuation circuit, while a pulsed e-beam was employed to measure the waveform (EVP) with reduced contamination or crosstalk. In the two cases provided in this article, EVI/EVP successfully demonstrated the values of e-beam probing on A16 GAA(Gate All Around) device with BSPDN design.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0423 | Accelerated Prediction and Spatial Profiling of HEIP Degradation for Layout-Driven Reliability Analysis

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2026p0419 | Application of Synchronous Radio-frequency Modulation Based Lock-In Infrared Thermography

 As reliance on dense multi-die integration systems evolves amid the exponential growth in demand for AI-based architectures (accelerators with HBM stacks, 2.5/3D integration, etc.), there is an urgent need to scale fault isolation and debug techniques to keep pace with this growth. Moreover, demanding workloads on these complex packages place them under additional stress, accelerating failure mechanisms such as electromigration, thermomechanical fatigue, delamination at various interfaces, and warpage-driven failures. Lock-in thermography (LIT) has remained a widely used technique in this domain for accurately isolating defects non-destructively, primarily because it can detect subtle thermal signatures through multiple relatively thick layers of materials. This study expands on a novel application of LIT that uses RF signals to isolate electrical open signatures and discusses RF-based LIT applications for fault isolation in 2.5/3D packaging architectures.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2026p0669 | Microwave Induced Plasma Etching for Selective Organic and Silicon Deprocessing in Advanced Packaging

 The current study demonstrates a dual-mode MIP dry etching process providing a highly selective and damage-free materials deprocessing solution for advanced semiconductor packages. DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0563 | 3D-VNAND Depth Measurement Using OFI Lock-In OBIRCH

 To increase yield and cost competitiveness, FLASH memory device developed vertical stack 3D-VNAND. Due to such high stacking and high integration, it is becoming increasingly difficult to identify the location of failures in vertical structures. DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0495 | Application of NAND FLASH Failure Analysis Using Visible Thermo Reaction

 In recent years, semiconductor devices have become more three-dimensional, and NAND FLASH devices are one of them. This trend is primarily driven by the need to pack more memory into a smaller footprint while reducing chip size and costs. Conventional analysis methods such as photoemission analysis and OBIRCH (Optical Beam Induced Resistance Change) analysis using an infrared light source are reaching their limits. As the NAND Height increases, it becomes more difficult for the Top & Back laser to reach, which reduces the signal and makes it harder to distinguish from noise signals, ultimately making it impossible to image the defective points. Thermal emission analysis that captures heat sources is one promising analysis method. However, the spatial resolution is insufficient, and optical beam based active thermal methods have been proposed to identify defective areas. Therefore, we focused on the thermos reflectance method and considered its application to the analysis of the VNAND (Vertical NAND). In this paper, we compare the conventional OBIRCH analysis with the new analysis method Visible Thermo Reaction (VTR) to demonstrate the effectiveness of the new method. We also introduce case studies and provide examples.

DOI 10.31399/asm.cp.istfa2025p0244 | Advancement Towards FIB Active Auto Thinning (AAT) Process

 In advanced DRAM and NAND structures, the feature size at the targeted cut face during the Focused Ion Beam (FIB) process has been reduced to sub-ten nanometer scale. Even with high-quality SEM resolution level scanned cut-face image data, determining the stopping point of the process remains a significant challenge. To address this, Micron has successfully implemented cut-face probability calculation and sliced image data interpolation methods using machine vision image artificial intelligence platforms (AIPs by Thermo Fisher Scientific) for the Micron’s latest memory devices analysis. This innovation enables machines to render a device’s three-dimensional geometry, infer and predict the upcoming cut face in SEM data points, and determine the stopping point in the FIB thinning process using detector’s raw data signal extractions. This approach avoids signal post-processing and transformation into visible light range for humans, thereby preventing meaningful data loss. The new methodology surpasses human eye detection limits, maximizes SEM resolution utilization, and converts an automation system into an autonomous system capable of deciding where to stop the FIB milling at each cut-face, based on the sensory input of Micron wafer’s cut-face analysis and learning algorithms. This technology has been integrated into its associated FIB platform, augmented by Open-AutoTEM, (iFast Extensibility). Micron defines this autonomous FIB thinning as active auto thinning (AAT), distinguishing it from the conventional fiducial-based FIB auto thinning, known as passive auto thinning (PAT).

DOI 10.1109/ITC58126.2025.00007 | Defect-Finding with Timing-Partitioned Small-Delay-Defect Methodology: Silicon Practice on N2
As semiconductor manufacturing processes continue to shrink, the impact of small-delay defects becomes increasingly significant. Traditional automatic test pattern generation (ATPG) methods often fail to detect these defects, as they are masked by critical paths. This work proposes a Timing-Partitioned Small-Delay-Defect (TPSDD) methodology to address this issue. By analyzing timing path delays and grouping transition paths based on their delay sizes, ATPG patterns can be generated to specifically test these groups, revealing hidden small-delay defects. Additionally, techniques for identifying outlier dies and defect candidates are presented, validated through testing on the N2 process node. Our silicon results confirm the effectiveness of TPSDD in detecting small-delay defects, enhancing reliability for applications requiring high robustness.

DOI 10.1109/itc58126.2025.00013 | Small Delay Defect Diagnosis via Timing-Aware Fault Simulation with Variant Delay Insertion

DOI 10.1109/ITC58126.2025.00035 | Efficient Delay Fault Characterization of Resistive Open Defects in Standard Cells Using Resistive Fault Dominance
Stringent quality requirements for safety-critical applications drive the demand for "zero defects" in modern ICs. In this context, delay characterization of standard cells for resistive open defects is an increasing concern due to aggressive timing margins in digital circuits. The problem is made worse by the large number of open defect sites in standard cells, combined with a wide range of defect resistance values for each site. This incurs possible prohibitive costs for defect simulation and characterization. To alleviate this complexity, we propose Resistive Fault Dominance (RFD) for resistive open defects. RFD eliminates simulations of certain open defects with intermediate defect resistance values that are guaranteed to exceed specified timing margins for standard cells, based on tests for specific "dominant" open defects. This can significantly reduce the computational costs of cell library characterization and simulation effort by 84%-91%. An algorithmic fault simulation methodology for resistive open defects on parasitic-extracted (PEX) transistor-level netlist is developed.

DOI 10.1109/itc58126.2025.00043 | Fault Modeling and Testing of Chiplet-to-Chiplet Interconnects in Fan-out Wafer-Level Packaging*
Advanced packaging technologies are reshaping; semiconductor integration, offering unprecedented improvements in performance, efficiency, and miniaturization. Among these Fan-Out Wafer-Level Packaging (FOWLP) has emerged as a transformative solution, pushing the limits of chiplet integration through its superior electrical performance, reduced footprint, and enhanced thermal management However, FOWLP presents several challenges, including coefficient of thermal expansion mismatch, warpage, die shift, and post-molding protrusion, all of which can lead to misalignment and defects. An embedded ring oscillator within the BIST network detects weak opens, bridging and coupling faults, quantifying the size of the defects. The effectiveness of the ring oscillator circuit is validated through HSPICE simulations using equivalent faulty circuit models for a 7 nm CMOS technology.

DOI 10.1109/ITC58126.2025.00019 | OCTANE: On-Chip Telemetry-based Anomaly Notification Engine
Silicon lifecycle management (SLM) is essential for ensuring the reliability and quality of silicon products. Traditional approaches primarily rely on off-chip solutions to detect malware, diagnose hardware bugs, and characterize silicon health metrics. However, these methods do not incorporate hardware/software co-design for SLM. This work introduces On- Chip Telemetry-based Anomaly Notification Engine (OCTANE), designed to monitor chip status using performance counters and sensors. OCTANE features a compute- and memory-efficient, unsupervised anomaly detection mechanism implemented on-chip (OCTANE-edge) using fixed-point arithmetic. Furthermore, it enhances on-chip anomaly detection through unsupervised feature ranking based on telemetry feature information entropy and compression index. This unsupervised feature ranking technique is workload-independent and provides the generalizability required for SLM. The proposed solution extends to an end-to-end anomaly-informed diagnosis model that leverages OCTANE-edge compacted anomaly telemetry signatures to diagnose chip security or safety incidents (OCTANE-cloud). OCTANE demonstrates highly effective unsupervised on-chip anomaly detection with minimal area and idle power overhead (1.2% and 2.6%). OCTANE provides anomaly detection/diagnosis with accuracy surpassing 0.96/0.98.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00085 | Memory Characterization Using Pattern Rank-Trajectory Analysis (PRTA) of Frequency–Voltage (Shmoo) Tests
Frequency–voltage (F–V) Shmoo characterization produces multi-dimensional failure data that per-condition Pareto analysis cannot exploit. We present Pattern Rank-Trajectory Analysis (PRTA), a framework that tracks failure-pattern rank movement between all-fail and first-fail Paretos across temperature and memory type. Each pattern is reduced to a two-dimensional trajectory—rank change at low versus high temperature—and classified into one of four quadrants with a direct engineering action. Applied to ∼14 million rows of Intel high-volume manufacturing data, PRTA replaces ∼600 Shmoo and Pareto artifacts with four quadrant assignments and narrows hundreds of patterns to fewer than ten with clear next steps, reducing a weeks-long manual analysis to about four hours on a single workstation while preserving engineering interpretability.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00045 | PyOTPL: A Python Abstraction Layer for Scalable, AI-Friendly ATE Test Program Development
Automated Test Equipment (ATE) program development using the OpenStar Test Program Language (OTPL) has traditionally relied on flat, non-programmatic module authoring, where routine changes are manual, repetitive, and error-prone. PyOTPL addresses this by allowing users to create their modules entirely from Python, with all the ease of use and expressiveness of a modern programming language. Additionally, the abstraction layer provides an insertion point for product-specific rules to be implemented in a way that is completely transparent to users, producing modules which are inherently compliant without requiring additional manual steps. The resulting Python source is an order of magnitude shorter than the equivalent OTPL, making it easier to review and maintain, while also drastically reducing AI token cost. Due to the ubiquity of Python on the internet and therefore in LLM training data, LLMs natively understand Python without additional context, as compared to OTPL, which is a specialized domain language that requires significant context for LLMs to reason over effectively. To that end, Module Agent, an agentic AI workflow built on top of PyOTPL, was created. Without the need for a full OTPL language server or additional context, Module Agent can output correct, complete modules in the shortest possible time and at the lowest token cost, either interactively or in the cloud. Together, PyOTPL and Module Agent demonstrate that the right abstraction layer can transform module authoring from a low-level editing task into an AI-friendly development workflow.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00023 | Scalable Timing-Aware Concurrent Fault Simulation for Transistor-Level DRAM Peripheral Circuits
This paper presents a concurrent fault simulation methodology tailored for timing-critical and transistor-level memory peripheral circuits. Existing concurrent fault simulators support only simplified timing abstractions, and extending them to annotated delay models poses two challenges: temporal divergence may occur without logic value divergence, and delay selection depends on passive state that lacks explicit fanout for event-driven tracking. The proposed method addresses these challenges by extending divergence tracking to a two-dimensional space spanning both logic values and temporal state, independently maintaining passive contributors within faulty machines, and establishing hierarchical port fault semantics with injection flags that constrain fault effects within switch-level hierarchies. Validation on industrial DRAM circuits demonstrates an average simulation speedup of approximately 44× while maintaining a coverage delta of at most 0.17% compared to the serial simulation mode.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00114 | Evolutionary Techniques for High-Level Source of Functional Test Programs

To address this, the proposed methodology uses an evolutionary engine to mutate the abstract-syntax tree representation of high-level functional test code targeting SDCs for increasing the repeatability of failing test programs. This approach explores diverse source code modifications derived from a common ancestor.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00086 | Temperature Management with Active Coolant Thermal Control (ACTC) in High-Power AI Chip Testing
This study presents Active Coolant Thermal Control (ACTC) for precise temperature regulation during enhanced voltage stress (EVS) testing of high-power AI chips. Using a Full Factorial Design (FFD), a predictive cooling model was developed and integrated into the prober system to dynamically adjust coolant flow rates. Statistical analysis via ANOVA $\left(R^{2} \geq 0.97\right)$ validated the accuracy of the proposed model. Implementation of ACTC successfully prevented thermal runaway and probe tip burn, significantly improving test yields and thermal stability over conventional passive PID control.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00046 | Closing the Debug Loop: An LLM Agent that Root Causes Silicon Failures
Post-silicon debug of complex processor failures remains one of the most time-intensive activities in semiconductor validation. This paper presents the Scandump Pass/Fail Analyzer, an AI-agent-based proof of concept that automates the ingestion, statistical analysis, and interpretation of scan dump data from silicon debug sessions. Applied to a real voltage-marginal sighting on a server-class processor, the analyzer autonomously identified the root-cause module and mechanism: a timing violation on an integrated clock gate (ICG) enable path. The hypothesis was driven by scan-data evidence and RTL-based design context, and was confirmed through silicon experiments proposed by the agent itself. We describe the analyzer's architecture, its application to the case study, and discuss implications for future agentic silicon debug workflows.

DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00027 | Industrial Experience for Achieving Advanced Test Coverage
Achieving high-quality test targets in advanced nodes is increasingly difficult as conventional test point insertion (TPI) saturates. This work evaluates hybrid TPI techniques and an enhanced test point (TP) structure developed with Synopsys to improve testability. DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00051 | AI-Empowered ArrayInsights: An Agentic Workflow for Layout-Aware MBIST Defect Analysis
Array memory defect analysis conventionally requires engineers to manually correlate MBIST raster failure data against physical layout coordinates and classify spatial defect patterns, which is a time-intensive and error-prone process. In this paper, AI-Empowered ArrayInsights is developed as an agentic AI workflow within an internal MCP server to automate this pipeline. Given MBIST raster data, $\text{AI}^{{2}}$ resolves logical-to-physical address mappings, classifies failures into eleven spatial defect categories, and generates memory-level and die-level layout heatmaps. DOI 10.1109/ITC64303.2026.00008 | An Interpretable, Production-Scale Machine-Learning Framework for Early CPU Performance Binning at the CP Stage
In advanced-node semiconductor manufacturing, the 1-3-month latency between chip probe (CP) wafer sort and final test (FT) delays detection of performance deviations, causing substantial yield loss of high-performance chips. We present an interpretable, production-scale LightGBM framework for early prediction of FT performance binning from CP-stage parameters (>5,000 features, $\mathbf{1 0}^{\mathbf{4}}-\mathbf{1 0}^{\mathbf{6}}$ chips/week), reducing the feedback loop from months to days. A two-stage feature selection pipeline (XGBoost → LightGBM) reduces dimensionality to 200 key parameters. Evaluated across six bin grouping schemes, the model reaches $\mathbf{A U C}-\mathbf{P R}=\mathbf{0. 9 3 8}$ on the Binary-HP task $(\mathbf{F 1}=\mathbf{0. 8 8 2})$; holdout-F1 ranges from 0.615 (7-class) to 0.882 (Binary-HP), a +43.4% spread that quantifies the accuracy-resolution trade-off. Group-specific feature importance profiling shows that different market segments depend on distinct physical drivers: voltage-regulator speed monitors and wafer-zone effects for high-performance classification; thermal diode features for mid-performance segmentation; and global timing clock driving force for top-tier binning. Compared with the industrial baseline of single chip-speed-monitor prediction $(\mathbf{A U C}-\mathbf{P R}=\mathbf{0. 5 8 3})$, the multi-feature model raises AUC-PR to 0.938 (+60.8% gain), improving overall test efficiency by enabling the elimination of redundant final-test iterations for predictable populations. Deployed at the CP sort station, the framework provides early warning within days rather than months, enabling production line adjustment to stabilize high-performance chip output. For advanced-node CPUs at HVM scale, reducing high-performance chip overkill via our CP-stage model yields ∼$5 million in cost savings per product generation.

DOI 10.1109/VLSITechnologyandCir65830.2026.11577576 | High Bandwidth Memory 4 (HBM4) Package Development Challenges
We report package development challenges associated with High Bandwidth Memory 4(HBM4) using Mechanical Test Vehicle 4 (MTV4): achieving 12-layer stacking and investigating stress interaction between HBM and advanced packaging technologies that have been used for Logic and HBM integration. Material and process innovations overcome challenges, such as die warpage and gap fill, caused by decreasing die thickness and reducing gap height. Micro-bump joint integrity for entire 12-layer stacks after reliability confirmed with daisy chain resistance and Confocal Scanning Acoustic Microscopy (C-SAM) analysis. The results indicated that test vehicle achieved void-free and high micro-bump integrity. Package interaction study is conducted with thermo-mechanical simulation methodology using identical floor plan. Higher stress is observed both at the HBM stacks (base die & core die) and at extremely lowk (ELK) layer when assembling on advanced substrate due to high coefficient of thermal expansion (CTE) mismatch. ELK stress at advanced substrate was as high as 2.5 x compared to Silicon interposer technology.
